# Test results

Build and Test for this piece of work: U5, U6 and U8, with the lanes of the earlier Units that U8 touches. This file records the stage's sixth run, after loop-back 4, on this Mac on 2026-11-23 from 03:20 to 05:26 UTC: the U8 chain from 03:20:24 to 04:34:16, the other lanes from 04:41:05 to 05:14:02, and the reruns from 05:17:56 to 05:25:42. The run's records were updated in place (Modify), not redone. Source: commit `bf82ed19` with the uncommitted work of U5 to U8; realm B's VerificationRun records the source state as uncommitted and unchanged during the run. Every U8 phase that records a source digest recorded `a23ec412…` over 2,267 files (17 evidence files in realm B, 14 in A, 3 in C and 8 in D), the digest that U8's checkpoint verification `0c9eee1c` recorded on 2026-11-23 from 02:51 to 03:07 UTC, before the maintainer approved U8. The one exception is `u2-regression`, whose source set adds U2's 31 test files to U8's; it records `c212fce2…` over 2,298 files. Target verdicts were assigned after the runs, from their evidence. An inventory finder and a skeptic first added the targets of loop-back 4's instruction sections. In each of eleven slices a checker judged every target and a skeptic challenged each verdict. A figures step gathered the run's figures, and a consistency critic read the slices against each other. Each group of rows the critic disputed went to a three-judge panel and was decided by majority; the two three-way splits were ruled by the coordinator. A script checked every citation of every Met row.

Rung 1's first step, `u6-ruff-ci-list`, failed again, as in run 5: it still lints run 4's list of U6's CI targets without the configurations U6's loop-back 3 added to its CI step, and gave the same 3 I001 findings in U6's scripts. U6's CI step itself, run exactly, passed (the row above), so the step is stale scaffolding, not a failing build; see "Failures and reruns". Not run here: `pnpm build`, the Next production build. The live v1 serves `frontend/.next` from this checkout, so CI's next-app job owns that build.

## Test results

Every final test run passed with no failed or skipped test, with these exceptions. U1's repository and integration phases ended exactly as decision L1-80 accepts, on U1's third fresh environment after two refused bootstraps. U7's lane passed on its fourth fresh environment after three refused setups. v1's root frontend suite failed 1 of 1,248 tests in one of its five runs. U8's schema compatibility check skipped itself by design, since no v2 tag exists yet. In rung 1, `ci.yml`'s eight suite jobs skipped what CI skips, while platform-integrity failed two tests and ops four in the sandbox under the umask 077 this stage keeps: the three umask-only tests passed when run again at CI's 022, and ops' three process-tree tests passed outside the sandbox. Those suites ran CI's ubuntu selection: the 31 platform-integrity cases and 10 ops tests gated to macOS were deselected. J14's repair exercise failed its planted-defect run by design before passing repaired. Coverage runs repeat the layer tests, so lane totals include repeats. The refusals, the root suite's failure and the stale lint step are under "Failures and reruns".

Commands that ran in more than one driver are counted once in the table above, and each result is stated: U6's coverage (949 passed and 89.64% both times), Ruff's seven invocations (clean both times), U8's ESLint and the root, e2e and U8 type checks (clean both times), U4's and U6's type checks and ESLint (three drivers each, clean each time), U5's type check and ESLint (clean both times), the root suite (failed 1 in `run-20261123-134106-22526`, passed in `s143-run-20261123-140811-47907`, then three times alone), U2's cumulative lane (754 three times), U3's and U4's Vitest lanes (71 and 108, twice each), the consumer inventories (passed each time) and U8's whole-folder collection (2,873 twice).

## Failures and reruns

1. **U8's checkpoint verification failed once at the corpus load, before the maintainer's approval.** On realm `docsuri-u8-8bd910258f8b851d`, VerificationRun `ddb0948b-3e6a-4820-9ab6-9934bfd0d595` ran from 02:42:25 to 02:45:43 UTC and ended incomplete: restore-paths (21 tests) and browser-recovery (J09) passed, then `fixtures --corpus only` exited 1 with "a synthetic corpus paper was refused: refused-by-database-time-limits" after 161 of 600 papers; J14 failed and the rest did not run. Read-only helper agents the coordinator had started 30 to 45 s earlier were running at the time. Causation is not proven, and the same refusal happened once before, at rung 1's Q5 measurement on 2026-11-19. The realm is kept as a failed run: `~/.docsuri-u8/final-evidence/docsuri-u8-preserved-docsuri-u8-8bd910258f8b851d-33cb0317`. By the maintainer's choice "Re-run fresh (Recommended)" the verification ran again alone on a fresh realm, `docsuri-u8-94cb48c0f4d32807`, with nothing else running, and passed: VerificationRun `0c9eee1c-92a0-4a8c-b0d8-c7efdb0c55b2`, 02:51:07–03:07:23 UTC, J01–J14, A11Y and SEC, source `a23ec412…`. The maintainer then approved U8. Run 6's realm B ran the same command and its corpus load completed in two passes, 383 and 217 papers. The refusal is a risk for every further realm B run.
2. **U1's bootstrap was refused twice, and U7's setup three times, each on a fresh environment, before each lane ran.** U1: in `run-20261123-135302-37965` and again in `run-20261123-141756-57816`, setup took 0.9 s and the bootstrap was refused 0.1 s later with exit 2, "Bootstrap refused: OperationalError; sqlstate=None"; the third attempt, `run-20261123-141927-59167`, set up in 1.8 s, bootstrapped in 0.4 s and ended exactly as L1-80 accepts. Through `u1_lane_capture.py`, both refused containers' logs were captured before teardown, and both show PostgreSQL's temporary init server restarting while the bootstrap connected: in the first, the init server was ready at 04:53:03.371, shut down at .521, and the real server was ready at .614; in the second, ready at 05:17:57.165, shut down at .321, real server ready at .413. This is the readiness race project.md's Testing Posture names: U1's setup waits with `docker exec pg_isready` over the container's Unix socket, which can pass against the image's socket-only init server, and the bootstrap then connects over TCP. U7: in `run-20261123-134106-22526` (04:52:26 UTC), `run-20261123-142135-60008` and `run-20261123-142307-60516`, setup was refused after 1.0 s with exit 2; the fourth attempt, `run-20261123-142439-61005`, passed in 1.7 s. No U7 container log exists: the first refused container was removed by the driver's teardown before it could be read (the Colima watch saw `docsuri-u7-7aa393471ff8e1e8` "Up 3 seconds" at 04:52:29 UTC), and for the other two the post-refusal read found "No such container". U7's tool waits the same way (`scripts/verify_docsuri_u7.py:211-229` and `:280-281`: `docker exec pg_isready`, then the bootstrap child at once), so the same race is the likely cause, but no log confirms it. All five refused environments are kept as failed prerequisites, not relabelled (see "Sources"). Run 5 had two such refusals, both U1's; run 6 had five. A setup that waits over TCP is a change to U1's and U7's tools, approved Units' files, and waits for the maintainer.
3. **v1's root frontend suite failed 1 of 1,248 tests in one of its five runs, on a race inside U6's own test.** In `run-20261123-134106-22526/071` (04:52:45 UTC, 9.79 s), straight after the realm lanes, the suite ended "1 failed | 1247 passed (1248)", exit 1: `test/u6-export/outcome-step.test.tsx` > "states before and after the write" > "says plainly when the export already started and cannot be cancelled", "Unable to find an element by: [data-testid="outcome-notice"]", a `findByTestId` wait at line 173 that timed out. It is not L1-44's case, so the driver did not rerun it. The same suite passed in `s143-run-20261123-140811-47907/018` and three times alone (`root-suite-alone-1.log` to `-3.log`), 1,248 each time. A judge traced the failure to a race inside the test, and the files confirm it: the fixture's effect starts in `accepted` (`frontend/test/u6-export/fixtures.ts:301`); the test mounts the step with `pollMs={15}` (`outcome-step.test.tsx:22`) and its read double answers `executing`; and `frontend/src/u6-export/outcome-step.tsx:165-173` renders the cancel button only while the state is `accepted`. When the 15 ms poll lands before `userEvent`'s click, the button is gone, `cancel()` never runs, and no notice appears. Loop-back 4 did not change the file. Across run 6 the test ran nine times and failed once (`chunk-u6b.json`). Swap used went from 8,718.94 MB at the driver's start (04:41:06 UTC) to 12,016.12 MB of 13,312 MB, read just after the driver ended (04:53:02 UTC), not at the failure. Nothing in the log ties the failure to memory.
4. **Rung 1's first step was stale, as in run 5.** `rung1-20261123-141248-53310/01` (`u6-ruff-ci-list`) exited 1 with the same 3 I001 findings at `scripts/serve_docsuri_u6.py:18`, `scripts/verify_docsuri_u6.py:40` and `:687`. The step still lints run 4's list of U6's CI targets without the configurations U6's loop-back 3 added. U6's CI step run exactly as its workflow writes it replaced the step: `u6-step63-extra-20261123-050725/ci-lint.log`, exit 0, "All checks passed!" four times. The driver step itself was not changed; no target depends on it.
5. **`ci.yml`'s platform-integrity and ops suites failed only in the sandbox and under umask 077, and the failing tests passed where CI runs them.** Platform-integrity had 2 failed, 460 passed, 185 skipped and 31 deselected: the two protected-receipt tests ("receipt output directory is unprotected"; a regex that did not match), because umask 077 masks the receipt folder's required 0o755 to 0o700. Ops had 4 failed, 375 passed, 5 skipped and 10 deselected: its three process-tree tests, which read the process table through `/bin/ps`, which no sandbox can run, and the Colima-executor test, whose expected refusal does not happen under 077. Step 20 ran the three process-tree tests outside the sandbox: 3 passed. Steps 21 and 22 ran the three umask-only tests at CI's umask 022: 2 passed and 1 passed. These are the same failures and the same reruns as run 5. The other six suites passed.
6. **Results by design or recorded only:** the schema compatibility check skipped itself until a v2 tag exists; J14's repair exercise failed 2 of 3 tests with its planted defect (exit 1, traced to `navigation.resolved` at `u8_routes.navigation_items`) and passed 3 of 3 repaired; the arrangement folder's collection exits 5 ("no tests collected"), which the driver counts as passed; U1's Vite build is owed to U1's CI lane; and the capacity burst, which judges nothing, answered six 502s (`DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`) in its third run at 0 streams, three on capabilities and three on the session, where run 5 recorded three at 4 streams.
7. **The Python dependency audit did not run**, as recorded above.
8. **The J12 digest observations, read correctly.** U8's code summary (lines 4493, 6785 and 7076) says the J12 digest observations "were not read": the coordinator had read the old table `operations.observation`, which is empty. Realm B was read again before its archive from the right table, `operations.observation_record` (`~/.docsuri-u8/final-evidence/bt6-realm-b-reading-20261123.txt`, read at 03:40:11 UTC by the database clock): 3,201 rows, the old table 0. The `topics.digest` capability was available 155 times; degraded once with `digest-delivery-late` at realm time 2026-11-24 05:02:22 and available again 5 s later; and unknown once with `producer-failed` at 05:07:47, available again the same second. The reading file gives no cause for the `producer-failed` observation, and it was not traced. 7 digest runs were sent, none without `scheduled_at`, and no incident was open. The checkpoint realm's reading has the same shape: 2,212 rows, available 105 times, one `digest-delivery-late` at 2026-11-24 04:32:54, available at 04:32:59, one `producer-failed` at 04:38:19, then available; 7 runs sent, none without `scheduled_at`; no incident. This corrects U8's records here; U8's code summary is not edited, since an edit would reopen U8.

## Measured figures

- **Performance** (realm B; 20 users for 60 seconds; 3.69 requests a second; no refused request; 64 readiness samples, all ready; the full 600-paper corpus, which the verification run's two corpus passes imported, 383 papers and then 217, before the performance phase). 95th percentiles in ms, against targets: search 279.2 (3,000), API reads 100 (500), first load 165 (2,000), accept 109 (1,000), navigation 134 (1,000), health 10.4 (200). The API-read figure is the host's read-class bucket. The host's own p95 buckets: accept 100, health 50, navigation 100, read 100, search 300, static 25. The result is recorded with `counts: false`: it did not run from `releases/<tag>/` at an installation.
- **The read class counts GET reads**: the host counted 147 requests in its read class against the 120 reads its users were answered (60 GET session reads and 60 POST settings reads), so the class check passed, and 6 in its static class. The load's own timing of those 120 reads gives a p95 of 30.3 ms. No GET-only p95 exists, because the load times both kinds together.
- **SCRAM logins**: 220 logins by 11 owners, 20 each, none failed; p50 6.94 ms, p95 9.71 ms, the first 13.66 ms, against a 20 ms bound on the p95, so the pool is not to be reconsidered. The slowest login took 30.70 ms, above 20 ms (run 5's slowest: 11.04 ms). They ran over Colima's forwarded port, not native loopback, so the counting figure falls to Performance Validation.
- **Backup** of the 1,571,781,655-byte database: 82.8 s, against the backup protocol's deadline of 1,800 s.
- **Capacity burst** (record only, U8's CP-6): 20 users' first loads at once, three runs each with 0, 4 and 8 job streams open. Users whose first load did not complete in full: 18 in each run with no stream open, 19 with 4, 20 with 8. Over the three runs at each size, ControlAdmission (8 normal and 2 recovery slots) refused session reads 59, 58 and 46 times and navigation 7, 32 and 128 times (`503 capacity.exhausted`), and the jobs list 3 and 6 times at 4 and 8 streams. uvicorn's concurrency limit answered 20 requests with a bare 503 ("Exceeded concurrency limit."): capabilities 7 and session 8 in the first run at 8 streams, capabilities 3 and session 2 in the second run at 4 streams (run 5: 32). The third run at 0 streams answered six 502s (`DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`), capabilities 3 and session 3 (run 5: three). No per-owner gate refused anything, every stream ended with its job, and readiness stayed ready in all 27 samples. These figures inform R-10's capacity choice.
- **Plans recorded, not judged**: with 60,000 embedded chunks, the meaning query did not use its HNSW index (a sequential scan), as in runs 4 and 5. The words query no longer used its GIN index `version_lexical_all`: run 5 recorded a bitmap index scan on it, and run 6 records a sequential scan. The first dry run checks the plans at the real size.
- **Q5's vector storage**, read by the performance phase's own `vectorStorage` reading on realm B, new in loop-back 4: at exactly 60,000 chunks, the single-precision `vector(1024)` values total 246,000,000 B and the HNSW index 491,528,192 B: together 737,528,192 B, 0.74 GB, 47.5% above the design's "about 0.5 GB". The phase's check `vector-storage-past-its-figure` passed, because it checks the maintainer's corrected bound, 774,404,601 B (105% of 12,292 B per chunk at 60,000 chunks plus 8,192 B; L4-2 (a), correction 193), with 36,876,409 B to spare, not the design's "about 0.5 GB", which no file has changed. The kept double-precision array adds 492,720,000 B and the projection table holds 1,038,655,488 B. The database (1,571,781,655 B) and its write-ahead log (939,524,096 B) total 2,511,305,751 B, inside the 8 GiB budget of 8,589,934,592 B (`footprint-past-its-budget` passed).
- **Memory**: the performance phase's load peaked at 697,581,568 B across five processes, and its browser at 796,868,608 B. The verification run's highest peak was 996,196,352 B, in the accessibility phase: 92.78% of the 1 GiB limit (1,073,741,824 B), above the 90% split line (966,367,641 B) by 29,828,711 B and under the limit by 77,545,472 B. It passed. Every other phase of the run stayed under the split line. Run 5's same phase peaked at 1,024,049,152 B (95.37%), and in U8's checkpoint verification `0c9eee1c` it peaked at 1,017,020,416 B (94.72%), above the line by 50,652,775 B. U5's and U6's browser lanes peaked at 998,014,976 B and 988,872,704 B, also above that line, which is U8's own split rule; U4's at 931,528,704 B. The capacity burst peaked at 686,440,448 B. Realm A's coverage-component-2 part rose from run 5's 672,694,272 B to 894,894,080 B, under the split line.
- **Disk and swap**: realm B started with 28.91 GiB free (31,041,093,632 B at its setup), above `build_realm.py`'s 22 GiB floor, and ended its phases with 25.46 GiB. Realms A, C and D started with 24.94, 25.60 and 25.29 GiB. During the performance phase the host had 28,213,350,400 B free. The U8 chain ended with 25.23 GiB free, the lanes chain with 22.02 GiB and the reruns with 19.90 GiB; the lowest reading was 18.97 GiB, at U1's third attempt (05:19:27 UTC). Swap went from 9,847.50 MB used of 11,264 MB at the U8 chain's start to 8,766.94 MB of 10,240 MB at its end, 12,016.12 MB of 13,312 MB after the first lanes driver, 14,022.88 MB of 15,360 MB at the lowest disk reading, and 12,954.06 MB of 14,336 MB at the end of the reruns.

## Target Verification Matrix

606 targets: run 5's 584, from every NFR requirement and design file of U5, U6 and U8, each Unit's Testing Contract and unit-test instructions, and U8's plan Steps 43 and 44 with its completion and evidence boundaries; and 22 new targets from the loop-back 4 sections of U5's and U8's unit-test instructions (U5 8, U8 14). Those sections also replaced the Expected value of 5 existing targets. No NFR file changed since run 5, so no other target was added or removed (`bt-matrix/bt6-inventory.json`).

Every row was checked again against this run's evidence and the current tests. The re-check (workflow `wf_c04cf406-ccb`, 26 agents on Opus, 6,631,626 tokens) ran an inventory finder and an inventory skeptic, then, in eleven slices, a checker who judged every target and a skeptic who challenged every verdict, then a figures step and a consistency critic. Before the panels the slices held Met 450, Not Met 9, Unverified this stage 31, Unverified later stage 112 and N/A 4. The critic checked all 1,764 citations of the 450 Met rows by script and found no bad citation. It raised eight contradictions, decided as follows:

1. U8's NFR9.2 and its export-and-digest row were Met, while the new required case for export was Not Met on the same code. A panel (3 of 3) kept both NFR9.2 rows Met and the required case Not Met: the requirement is met in the code and runs, while the required case names a test that does not exist.
2. U8's NFR3.3 and its libpq row handed their last clause to Environment Provisioning, while the new required case for NFR3.3 was Not Met on a defect in the same installation check. A panel (3 of 3) put both NFR3.3 rows with Environment Provisioning and kept the required case Not Met.
3. U8's NFR1.3 admission row and the new required case for NFR1.3 judged the same unbuilt window two ways. A panel ruled NFR1.3 itself Not Met (3 of 3), the admission row Unverified owned by Deployment Execution (2 of 3; the literal judge ruled it Not Met on the consent, setup and signed-in pages) and the required case Not Met (3 of 3).
4. U8's Step 43 browser isolation and the new required case for it were Not Met on different grounds, while BR10.3's journeys row was Met on the same journey. A panel kept Step 43 Not Met (3 of 3), the required case Not Met (3 of 3) and BR10.3's journeys row Met (2 of 3; the evidence judge ruled it Not Met, because J03 is not a row of Step 168(a)'s table, which the maintainer's answer covers).
5. The NFR9.2 notes named the restart-database phase as the cause of the `producer-failed` observation, while another note called it untraced. The coordinator's updates corrected the notes: the reading file gives no cause, and it was not traced.
6. Rows applied different standards to rules about how tests are written. A panel (2 of 3) set the standard: such a rule is Met only when a test, scan or recorded run enforces it, and a reading alone is Unverified owned by this stage. Under it, U8's never-mocked row became Unverified (2 of 3), and U5's two new rows, never-mocked for the run schedule and the clock's pass instant, became Unverified (2 of 3 each). U8's new loop-back 4 clock row split three ways (Not Met, Met, Unverified), and the coordinator ruled it Unverified (this stage). U8's environment-tool failure rules and U5's no-false-success row stayed Met (3 of 3 each), because a recorded run enforces their added clause.
7. U5's, U6's and U8's rows on the existing suites read the root suite's one failure differently. A panel ruled U6's existing-suites row Not Met (2 of 3) and U8's scope floor Not Met (2 of 3). U5's scope-floor-green row split three ways (Not Met, Unverified, Met if the test is outside its regressions), and the coordinator ruled it Not Met, because its Expected begins "Existing suites stay green".
8. Phase durations were quoted two ways. The coordinator's updates give the evidence files' durations with the chain's wall clock beside them: performance 155.0 s (156.0 s by the chain), capacity burst 40.4 s (41.4 s).

A seventh panel group, U8's P2 search-model-resident row, came from the critic's list of changes on unchanged code; the panel ruled it Not Met (3 of 3). The panels (workflow `wf_cc3dfdb7-025`, 21 agents, seven groups of three judges, each reading through a literal, a precedent and an evidence lens, decided by majority, 3,495,460 tokens) are in `bt-matrix/judge-panel-wf_cc3dfdb7-025.json`. The coordinator's updates to 30 rows, from the panels' rulings and the judges' factual corrections (verdicts, panel counts, durable evidence paths, durations), were written, checked and fixed by workflow `wf_93014927-1dc` (621,711 tokens) into `bt-matrix/bt6-lead-updates.json` and applied with `apply_slice_updates.py`; six of them change a verdict.

One inconsistency is left visible on purpose. The panel's standard for rules about how tests are written was applied to the six rows the panel saw. Other rows that run 5 ruled Met on a full reading, for example U6's never-mocked row, keep run 5's standard, because sweeping them would have changed the procedure the maintainer asked to keep. A re-check that applied the standard everywhere could move more Met rows to Unverified; how many is not counted.

A "Met" names the test, measured figure or run that checks the target in full. A script confirmed every test cited by a Met row: the file exists, the test is in it and the quoted line appears in it verbatim, with no problem found (`bt-matrix/verify-report.txt`, after the coordinator's updates). Seventeen Met rows rest on a run rather than a test, and each was re-read by hand against its run-6 log's exit line and figures, or its evidence file's exit code and counts (`bt-matrix/run-evidence-checked.json`). This run applies the browser rule of the 2.11.3 runtime's copy of the stage file (`~/.local/share/aidlc/versions/2.11.3/runtime/claude/.claude/aidlc-common/stages/construction/build-and-test.md`), as runs 4 and 5 did: a check in Node or jsdom is integration evidence, and only a run in a real browser engine, here Chromium driven by Playwright, makes a target that needs browser behaviour Met. The project's installed copy, which this run's directive names (`.claude/aidlc-common/stages/construction/build-and-test.md`), has neither this rule nor its exception for the gate (see "Outcome").

### Verdicts changed on code loop-back 4 did not change

Each changed row carries a flag for whether loop-back 4 changed the code under it. Four are flagged "no" and two "partly".

- **"No": U5's scope-floor-green, U6's existing suites and U8's scope floor.** No code under them changed. What changed is the evidence: in one of five runs of the root suite, a test that loop-back 4 did not touch, U6's `outcome-step.test.tsx`, failed on a race inside the test ("Failures and reruns", item 3), and the panel ruled that an existing suite red on a valid setup. Run 5 ran the suite once, and it passed.
- **"No": U8's P2 search-model-resident row.** `backend/modules/operations/producers.py:1044-1048` posts the same empty input as in runs 3 to 5, which noted the mismatch and parked it with residency. What changed is the reading: run 6's checker read the design line, "The search probe repeats a one-word embedding with keep_alive: -1 every 60 seconds" (`performance-design.md:122`), against the code, and all three judges say plainly that this is a verdict change on unchanged code.
- **"Partly": U8's NFR1.3.** The changed part: loop-back 4's Steps 169-170 built the Chromium checks of `/search` and the proof forms, which closed run 5's two in-stage parts. The unchanged part, which now makes the row Not Met: the email cancellation page, `GET /api/v2/topics/email-unsubscribe/{token}`, mounted on U8's host (`backend/u8_outward.py:501-521`) and served by U5's `backend/modules/topics/unsubscribe.py:237-247`, which no lap changed and run 5's row did not name.
- **"Partly": U8's never-mocked row.** The changed part: loop-back 4 replaced its Expected, adding six never-mocked items (U8 `unit-test-instructions.md:1302-1308`), and added the tests those items speak of. The part that turns the verdict: `test_never_mocked.py`'s scan checks only the earlier list, and correction 213 records a stand-in's handle replaced in a way the scan does not see; under the panel's standard a reading alone is Unverified.

### Unverified, this stage

The 33 targets this stage owns but could not verify, by cause (`bt6-rung2.json` gives each one's root cause and fix):

- **Browser behaviour checked only in jsdom (26), the browser-only gaps for the approval gate.** For each, every clause the matrix calls unchecked here did run, but only in jsdom (or with Vitest fake timers or a stand-in EventSource), in no real browser engine, and nothing else is open in this stage. The 2.11.3 runtime's copy of the stage file (:225-228) says such a target at the approval gate (Step 12) rather than handling it as a failure; the project's installed copy has no such exception, so under it these 26 fail too ("Outcome"). They are the same 26 as in run 5: loop-back 4 built none of them (the maintainer's decisions L4-7 for U5 and L4-8 for U8; U6 was not reopened).
  - U5 (3): NFR5.1 (the topic list's and resolve control's live-region messages and the Korean names of their buttons), the frontend states and the preference inheritance (the defaults label).
  - U6 (6): D7's help, NFR3.4's connect field, NFR3.8's provider text, NFR5.1's component checks, the C09 client keys and the plaintext lifetime.
  - U8 (17): D3's provider-error return, NFR1.1 and its owner-change clearing, NFR5.1 with its status messages, its axe row and its focus and live region, NFR6.2, NFR7.12's other sign-in method, NFR9.2's observation buffer, NFR9.4 with its dashboard contents and refresh, NFR9.5 with its job view and jobs-list refresh, and S6.
- **Rules about how tests are written that no test, scan or run enforces (4), failing.** Each holds by a full reading, which under the panel's standard is not enough: U5's never-mocked rule for the run schedule and its clock rule for the pass instant (both new; U5's only scan checks logging uses and provider variables); U8's never-mocked row (its six added items are outside the scan); and U8's loop-back 4 clock row (the coordinator's ruling on a three-way split; the literal judge found hand-written instants at the topic's own 09:00 delivery time in `component/test_producers.py`, against the Expected's "Every hand-written instant differs from its window's day at the topic's current delivery time").
- **Order of work, which no test can show (3), failing.** U5's methodology layers and runner step, and U8's testable layers. Code Generation's records show the order in which code and tests were written; no test can.

On the production-shaped instance each of the eight holders is changed once, 19 roles in all: each role gets a new SCRAM-SHA-256$4096 verifier the step computes itself, its holder (file or keychain item) is rewritten with the new value, the new value logs in and the old one is refused, and a file keeps 0600, one name and its account. A failure midway leaves the holder as it was (verifier-not-set) or says holder-not-rewritten, and the word repairs it when run again. The word runs only as root, only for the installed operator named by SUDO_UID, and its sudo line sits only in DOCSURI_INPERSON_ROOT, never NOPASSWD. | U8:D8:secret-custody | U8 security-design > 6.1 Where each secret lives (D8, NFR3.2) > table | Each secret is held only by its one account in a mode-0600 file inside an owner-only 0700 folder, read with the private-file chec… | Each generated secret has exactly one holder: a 0600, single-named file given to its account through its own descriptor (owners and platform reader 610, scheduler 611, backup agent 607, provisioner root), or one of the four root keychain items. | U8:NFR7.16 | reliability-requirements > NFR7.16 | A rollback is possible for 14 days after admission opens. v2's withdrawals and erasures reach v1 before v1 serves, and v2's datab… | Checked in full. The window: a rollback is allowed at the opening's instant and at the record's own admissionOpenedAt plus exactly 14 days, on a cutover whose opening came 60 s after v1's stop (switchStartedAt before admissionOpenedAt), and is refused at 14 days and 1 s (rollback-window-closed); so it counts from the opening, includes its last instant, and is bounded neither by v1's stop nor by the 7-day soak. The 14 days are the production policy's own value (PRODUCTION_OPERATIONS.rollback_window_seconds, pinned field by field), and the synthetic policy the tests use keeps it unchanged. Settlement is refused through the same instant (cutover.py:792-795), and the opening sets v1's stores' disposal at the policy's 90 days (the tool refuses any other value, tool.py:937-940), so v1's stores outlast the window; L3-8's two recorded findings (the tool's unreachable before-window-end refusal, Cutover.window_open with no caller) do not narrow it. The operator tool's rollback goes through that rule (ops/src/docsuri_ops/cutover/tool.py:1298 calls Cutover.rollback, cutover.py:677-680). The late-window rollback is checked on the machine with an injected clock; the realm journey rolls back at the opening's instant. The outcomes: in the realm journey, with the synthetic v1 up, one migrated account erased and another withdrew in v2, and the rollback applied both through v1's own account code (v2_rollback_apply): in v1 both are DEACTIVATED, only the erasure has a deletion record, the evidence lists (erase, DEACTIVATED) and (withdraw, DEACTIVATED), every v1 session ended and v1-web served; the machine refuses v1 serving until outcomes are applied and sessions ended. Notion: while provider.notion is observed unavailable, an export answers DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE (cause provider-held) and no client, request or token is opened; once the probe passes, calls resume. Search: while provider.models is observed unavailable, a semantic search answers by words with semantic-search-unavailable and sends nothing to the model runtime, in a composed production too, even once the runtime answers again; after a passing probe semantic search resumes; unknown and stale fail open (L3-7). | U8:nfr-design-questions:circuit-breaker | U8 nfr-design-questions > Settled from the records > Providers' time limits | While a provider's capability is unavailable, the features that depend on it say so and do not call it, with no fallback (NFR7.12… | Each provider's observed capability state now works as its breaker at every call site U8's plan found (L4-11, Steps 161-162). Models: the breaker sits in the one runtime client, so tags, verify, infer, resident and pin_resident each refuse provider-held with no request while provider.models is observed unavailable; both availability answers (research's and assistance's executor) ask it afresh over any kept True answer, so the lane claims nothing: with an accepted research turn and an accepted assistance job and the runtime answering normally, three lane turns answer skipped for both owners, the research row stays accepted with 0 attempts and no request reaches the runtime, also when only the search model's digest is wrong (observed through the real producer), the case the generation model's own check cannot see; once the probe passes, the jobs run. Housekeeping closes no unclaimed job and asks the runtime nothing while held; the import stops provider-held with no embedding and research-review is refused before any runtime call. In a composed production, after an observed outage, the lane's turns, a workers pass and a semantic search send nothing, and the next observation's own request is the only one. Google and ORCID: a start writes no record and a pending flow's completion makes no token request while the provider is observed unavailable, the activation never moving. Resend: account mail is held up front, and a due digest stays prepared with no allowance reserved and nothing sent. Notion and search hold as in run 5 (no client, request or token; search answers by words, degraded, lexical, semantic-search-unavailable). unknown and stale fail open at every site; a state read that raises reads unknown. | Run 6 (realm A docsuri-u8-9f0c5afbdd0c3037, realm D docsuri-u8-363bf5941cce23f0, realm C docsuri-u8-2e7faf6d24fc5b95;
